Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure of the exact amount… I mean, student health has been tracking the number and they said it’s too low now to clear me for the youth workshops. They seemed worried because it’s kept dropping.\n\nTo me it didn’t feel like this huge intentional thing, exactly… it just feels like what happens when I’m really focused and busy, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be fat even if you weighed the same as other people your age and height?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d call it “fat,” exactly. That sounds really harsh.\n\nBut when I see photos of myself, like from the showcase, I don’t really notice the artwork first. I kind of immediately zoom in on my arms or my waist, and if I look small enough, I feel… relieved, I guess. Like I look more serious, more put-together.\n\nI know that probably isn’t the healthiest way to look at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour or two?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I don’t think so.\n\nI mean, sometimes if I’ve gone too long without eating, I might have more than I meant to, like a snack turns into a little more, but I wouldn’t say it’s a huge amount or anything. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics to try to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Um… no, not really. I don’t use those to try to lose weight or anything.\n\nI mean, I know what they are, but that’s not something I do. Is that… is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is low even when others tell you it is not?\n\nPatient: I mean… people do say that, yeah. They say it’s too low or that it’s dangerous, but I guess it feels kind of exaggerated to me.\n\nI don’t really see it that way. It feels more like everyone’s focusing on my body like it’s the problem, when I’m just trying to stay disciplined and sharp for school and my work. Is that… does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Did you intentionally try to lose weight?\n\nPatient: I… I guess I did, at least at first. I don’t know if I thought of it as “trying to lose weight” exactly. It felt more like trying to be in control.\n\nLike, I plan my day around not really eating much—coffee before studio, maybe a few bites of something from the café if other people are around. And I avoid bread or anything oily because it feels like it would kind of undo the control I worked for.\n\nThat sounds bad when I say it out loud, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you mean, I guess.\n\nLike, I’m usually pretty aware of what I’m eating. I might feel upset afterward if I had more than I planned, but it doesn’t feel like I’m out of control exactly. More like I didn’t do it “right,” if that makes sense.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that's definitely larger than what most people would eat under similar circumstances?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not definitely larger than what other people would eat.\n\nSometimes I worry something was “too much” for me, but if I try to compare it to a normal person, it probably wouldn’t look like some huge amount. I’m not sure if I’m answering that right.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that you couldn't stop or control?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I’ve felt guilty or anxious after eating, but not like I couldn’t stop myself.\n\nIf anything, I’m usually kind of watching it the whole time, maybe too much. They said they can’t clear me for the youth workshops right now, because the number has kept going down.\n\nI don’t really feel like it’s as dramatic as it maybe sounds—I’ve just been really focused and busy, and I guess it kind of happened. But they wanted me to talk to someone, so… I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I mean… I wouldn’t say I’m “afraid” in a dramatic way, but… yeah, I guess I do get really anxious about it. Even thinking about gaining a pound kind of makes me panic.\n\nLike if I eat a normal dinner, or something with butter in it, I start feeling like everyone will be able to tell I messed up… like they’ll see I lost discipline. I know that probably sounds bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you limit the amount or types of food you eat?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I do, yeah. I try not to make it a big deal, but I kind of plan my day around eating as little as I can.\n\nLike I’ll have coffee before studio, and then maybe if people are around I’ll get something at the café and have a few bites, so it doesn’t look weird. And I mostly avoid bread or anything oily… it just feels like that would undo all the control I’ve been trying to keep. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure of the exact amount… I mean, student health has been tracking the number and they said it’s too low now to clear me for the youth workshops. They seemed worried because it’s kept dropping.\n\nTo me it didn’t feel like this huge intentional thing, exactly… it just feels like what happens when I’m really focused and busy, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be fat even if you weighed the same as other people your age and height?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d call it “fat,” exactly. That sounds really harsh.\n\nBut when I see photos of myself, like from the showcase, I don’t really notice the artwork first. I kind of immediately zoom in on my arms or my waist, and if I look small enough, I feel… relieved, I guess. Like I look more serious, more put-together.\n\nI know that probably isn’t the healthiest way to look at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour or two?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I don’t think so.\n\nI mean, sometimes if I’ve gone too long without eating, I might have more than I meant to, like a snack turns into a little more, but I wouldn’t say it’s a huge amount or anything. At least, I don’t think that’s what you mean by “large,” right?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, not on purpose.\n\nI’ve felt nauseous before if I’m anxious or if I ate too fast, but I don’t, like, make myself throw up after eating. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics to try to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Um… no, not really. I don’t use those to try to lose weight or anything.\n\nI mean, I know what they are, but that’s not something I do. Is that… is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is low even when others tell you it is not?\n\nPatient: I mean… people do say that, yeah. They say it’s too low or that it’s dangerous, but I guess it feels kind of exaggerated to me.\n\nI don’t really see it that way. It feels more like everyone’s focusing on my body like it’s the problem, when I’m just trying to stay disciplined and sharp for school and my work. Is that… does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Did you intentionally try to lose weight?\n\nPatient: I… I guess I did, at least at first. I don’t know if I thought of it as “trying to lose weight” exactly. It felt more like trying to be in control.\n\nLike, I plan my day around not really eating much—coffee before studio, maybe a few bites of something from the café if other people are around. And I avoid bread or anything oily because it feels like it would kind of undo the control I worked for.\n\nThat sounds bad when I say it out loud, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you mean, I guess.\n\nLike, I’m usually pretty aware of what I’m eating. I might feel upset afterward if I had more than I planned, but it doesn’t feel like I’m out of control exactly. More like I didn’t do it “right,” if that makes sense.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that's definitely larger than what most people would eat under similar circumstances?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not definitely larger than what other people would eat.\n\nSometimes I worry something was “too much” for me, but if I try to compare it to a normal person, it probably wouldn’t look like some huge amount. I’m not sure if I’m answering that right.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that you couldn't stop or control?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I’ve felt guilty or anxious after eating, but not like I couldn’t stop myself.\n\nIf anything, I’m usually kind of watching it the whole time, maybe too much. So… no, I don’t think I’ve had that feeling of being unable to control it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your anxiety about gaining weight is one of the main things that drives you to restrict your food intake?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it probably is.\n\nI don’t want to sound dramatic, but even the idea of gaining, like, a pound can make me really anxious. If I eat a normal dinner or something with butter in it, I start thinking people will somehow be able to tell… like they’ll see I’ve lost discipline or something.\n\nI know that might not make sense, but that fear is a big part of why I hold back with food.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

It felt more like trying to be in control.\n\nLike, I plan my day around not really eating much—coffee before studio, maybe a few bites of something from the café if other people are around. And I avoid bread or anything oily because it feels like it would kind of undo the control I worked for.\n\nThat sounds bad when I say it out loud, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you mean, I guess.\n\nLike, I’m usually pretty aware of what I’m eating. I might feel upset afterward if I had more than I planned, but it doesn’t feel like I’m out of control exactly. More like I didn’t do it “right,” if that makes sense.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that's definitely larger than what most people would eat under similar circumstances?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not definitely larger than what other people would eat.\n\nSometimes I worry something was “too much” for me, but if I try to compare it to a normal person, it probably wouldn’t look like some huge amount. I’m not sure if I’m answering that right.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that you couldn't stop or control?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I’ve felt guilty or anxious after eating, but not like I couldn’t stop myself.\n\nIf anything, I’m usually kind of watching it the whole time, maybe too much. So… no, I don’t think I’ve had that feeling of being unable to control it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your anxiety about gaining weight is one of the main things that drives you to restrict your food intake?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it probably is.\n\nI don’t want to sound dramatic, but even the idea of gaining, like, a pound can make me really anxious. If I eat a normal dinner or something with butter in it, I start thinking people will somehow be able to tell… like they’ll see I’ve lost discipline or something.\n\nI know that might not make sense, but that fear is a big part of why I hold back with food.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time, and then felt really upset or guilty about it?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I definitely feel upset or guilty after eating sometimes, but I wouldn’t say it’s after a large amount in a short period of time.\n\nIt’s more like even a normal amount can feel wrong to me, if that makes sense. But I don’t think I’ve had what you’re describing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I try not to make it a big deal, but I kind of plan my day around eating as little as I can.\n\nLike I’ll have coffee before studio, and then maybe if people are around I’ll get something at the café and have a few bites, so it doesn’t look weird. And I mostly avoid bread or anything oily… it just feels like that would undo all the control I’ve been trying to keep. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure of the exact amount… I mean, student health has been tracking the number and they said it’s too low now to clear me for the youth workshops. They seemed worried because it’s kept dropping.\n\nTo me it didn’t feel like this huge intentional thing, exactly… it just feels like what happens when I’m really focused and busy, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be fat even if you weighed the same as other people your age and height?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d call it “fat,” exactly. I kind of immediately zoom in on my arms or my waist, and if I look small enough, I feel… relieved, I guess. Like I look more serious, more put-together.\n\nI know that probably isn’t the healthiest way to look at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour or two?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I don’t think so.\n\nI mean, sometimes if I’ve gone too long without eating, I might have more than I meant to, like a snack turns into a little more, but I wouldn’t say it’s a huge amount or anything. I guess… yes, but I don’t like saying it that directly.

It’s not only “don’t gain weight.” I think there is this part of me that wants to look… small. Like, when I look at photos from a showcase or something, I don’t really look at the artwork first, which sounds awful. I zoom in on my arms and my waist, and if I look small enough, I feel like I seem more serious or put-together.

I know that probably sounds shallow. I’m not trying to be vain, but… yeah, I think being thin feels like proof I’m doing something right. Is that the kind of thing you mean?

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.0, F50.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, hi. So… student health told me I needed to come in because they’re concerned my weight is too low. They said they can’t clear me for the youth workshops right now, because the number has kept going down.\n\nI don’t really feel like it’s as dramatic as it maybe sounds—I’ve just been really focused and busy, and I guess it kind of happened. But they wanted me to talk to someone, so… I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I mean… I wouldn’t say I’m “afraid” in a dramatic way, but… yeah, I guess I do get really anxious about it. Even thinking about gaining a pound kind of makes me panic.\n\nLike if I eat a normal dinner, or something with butter in it, I start feeling like everyone will be able to tell I messed up… like they’ll see I lost discipline. I know that probably sounds bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you limit the amount or types of food you eat?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I do, yeah. I try not to make it a big deal, but I kind of plan my day around eating as little as I can.\n\nLike I’ll have coffee before studio, and then maybe if people are around I’ll get something at the café and have a few bites, so it doesn’t look weird. And I mostly avoid bread or anything oily… it just feels like that would undo all the control I’ve been trying to keep. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure of the exact amount… I mean, student health has been tracking the number and they said it’s too low now to clear me for the youth workshops. They seemed worried because it’s kept dropping.\n\nTo me it didn’t feel like this huge intentional thing, exactly… it just feels like what happens when I’m really focused and busy, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be fat even if you weighed the same as other people your age and height?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d call it “fat,” exactly. That sounds really harsh.\n\nBut when I see photos of myself, like from the showcase, I don’t really notice the artwork first. I kind of immediately zoom in on my arms or my waist, and if I look small enough, I feel… relieved, I guess. Like I look more serious, more put-together.\n\nI know that probably isn’t the healthiest way to look at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour or two?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I don’t think so.\n\nI mean, sometimes if I’ve gone too long without eating, I might have more than I meant to, like a snack turns into a little more, but I wouldn’t say it’s a huge amount or anything. At least, I don’t think that’s what you mean by “large,” right?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, not on purpose.\n\nI’ve felt nauseous before if I’m anxious or if I ate too fast, but I don’t, like, make myself throw up after eating. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics to try to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Um… no, not really. I don’t use those to try to lose weight or anything.\n\nI mean, I know what they are, but that’s not something I do. Is that… is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is low even when others tell you it is not?\n\nPatient: I mean… people do say that, yeah. They say it’s too low or that it’s dangerous, but I guess it feels kind of exaggerated to me.\n\nI don’t really see it that way. It feels more like everyone’s focusing on my body like it’s the problem, when I’m just trying to stay disciplined and sharp for school and my work. Is that… does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Did you intentionally try to lose weight?\n\nPatient: I… I guess I did, at least at first. I don’t know if I thought of it as “trying to lose weight” exactly. It felt more like trying to be in control.\n\nLike, I plan my day around not really eating much—coffee before studio, maybe a few bites of something from the café if other people are around. And I avoid bread or anything oily because it feels like it would kind of undo the control I worked for.\n\nThat sounds bad when I say it out loud, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you mean, I guess.\n\nLike, I’m usually pretty aware of what I’m eating. I might feel upset afterward if I had more than I planned, but it doesn’t feel like I’m out of control exactly. More like I didn’t do it “right,” if that makes sense.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that's definitely larger than what most people would eat under similar circumstances?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not definitely larger than what other people would eat.\n\nSometimes I worry something was “too much” for me, but if I try to compare it to a normal person, it probably wouldn’t look like some huge amount. I’m not sure if I’m answering that right.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that you couldn't stop or control?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I’ve felt guilty or anxious after eating, but not like I couldn’t stop myself.\n\nIf anything, I’m usually kind of watching it the whole time, maybe too much. So… no, I don’t think I’ve had that feeling of being unable to control it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your anxiety about gaining weight is one of the main things that drives you to restrict your food intake?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it probably is.\n\nI don’t want to sound dramatic, but even the idea of gaining, like, a pound can make me really anxious. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, hi. So… student health told me I needed to come in because they’re concerned my weight is too low. They said they can’t clear me for the youth workshops right now, because the number has kept going down.\n\nI don’t really feel like it’s as dramatic as it maybe sounds—I’ve just been really focused and busy, and I guess it kind of happened. But they wanted me to talk to someone, so… I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I mean… I wouldn’t say I’m “afraid” in a dramatic way, but… yeah, I guess I do get really anxious about it. Even thinking about gaining a pound kind of makes me panic.\n\nLike if I eat a normal dinner, or something with butter in it, I start feeling like everyone will be able to tell I messed up… like they’ll see I lost discipline. I know that probably sounds bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you limit the amount or types of food you eat?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I do, yeah. I try not to make it a big deal, but I kind of plan my day around eating as little as I can.\n\nLike I’ll have coffee before studio, and then maybe if people are around I’ll get something at the café and have a few bites, so it doesn’t look weird. And I mostly avoid bread or anything oily… it just feels like that would undo all the control I’ve been trying to keep. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure of the exact amount… I mean, student health has been tracking the number and they said it’s too low now to clear me for the youth workshops. They seemed worried because it’s kept dropping.\n\nTo me it didn’t feel like this huge intentional thing, exactly… it just feels like what happens when I’m really focused and busy, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be fat even if you weighed the same as other people your age and height?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d call it “fat,” exactly. That sounds really harsh.\n\nBut when I see photos of myself, like from the showcase, I don’t really notice the artwork first. I kind of immediately zoom in on my arms or my waist, and if I look small enough, I feel… relieved, I guess. Like I look more serious, more put-together.\n\nI know that probably isn’t the healthiest way to look at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour or two?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I don’t think so.\n\nI mean, sometimes if I’ve gone too long without eating, I might have more than I meant to, like a snack turns into a little more, but I wouldn’t say it’s a huge amount or anything. At least, I don’t think that’s what you mean by “large,” right?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, not on purpose.\n\nI’ve felt nauseous before if I’m anxious or if I ate too fast, but I don’t, like, make myself throw up after eating. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics to try to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Um… no, not really. I don’t use those to try to lose weight or anything.\n\nI mean, I know what they are, but that’s not something I do. Is that… is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is low even when others tell you it is not?\n\nPatient: I mean… people do say that, yeah. They say it’s too low or that it’s dangerous, but I guess it feels kind of exaggerated to me.\n\nI don’t really see it that way. It feels more like everyone’s focusing on my body like it’s the problem, when I’m just trying to stay disciplined and sharp for school and my work. Is that… does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Did you intentionally try to lose weight?\n\nPatient: I… I guess I did, at least at first. I don’t know if I thought of it as “trying to lose weight” exactly. I’m not sure if I’m answering that right.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that you couldn't stop or control?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I’ve felt guilty or anxious after eating, but not like I couldn’t stop myself.\n\nIf anything, I’m usually kind of watching it the whole time, maybe too much. So… no, I don’t think I’ve had that feeling of being unable to control it. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, hi. So… student health told me I needed to come in because they’re concerned my weight is too low. They said they can’t clear me for the youth workshops right now, because the number has kept going down.\n\nI don’t really feel like it’s as dramatic as it maybe sounds—I’ve just been really focused and busy, and I guess it kind of happened. But they wanted me to talk to someone, so… I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I mean… I wouldn’t say I’m “afraid” in a dramatic way, but… yeah, I guess I do get really anxious about it. Even thinking about gaining a pound kind of makes me panic.\n\nLike if I eat a normal dinner, or something with butter in it, I start feeling like everyone will be able to tell I messed up… like they’ll see I lost discipline. I know that probably sounds bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you limit the amount or types of food you eat?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I do, yeah. I try not to make it a big deal, but I kind of plan my day around eating as little as I can.\n\nLike I’ll have coffee before studio, and then maybe if people are around I’ll get something at the café and have a few bites, so it doesn’t look weird. And I mostly avoid bread or anything oily… it just feels like that would undo all the control I’ve been trying to keep. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure of the exact amount… I mean, student health has been tracking the number and they said it’s too low now to clear me for the youth workshops. They seemed worried because it’s kept dropping.\n\nTo me it didn’t feel like this huge intentional thing, exactly… it just feels like what happens when I’m really focused and busy, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be fat even if you weighed the same as other people your age and height?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d call it “fat,” exactly. That sounds really harsh.\n\nBut when I see photos of myself, like from the showcase, I don’t really notice the artwork first. I kind of immediately zoom in on my arms or my waist, and if I look small enough, I feel… relieved, I guess. Like I look more serious, more put-together.\n\nI know that probably isn’t the healthiest way to look at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like within an hour or two?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I don’t think so.\n\nI mean, sometimes if I’ve gone too long without eating, I might have more than I meant to, like a snack turns into a little more, but I wouldn’t say it’s a huge amount or anything. At least, I don’t think that’s what you mean by “large,” right?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, not on purpose.\n\nI’ve felt nauseous before if I’m anxious or if I ate too fast, but I don’t, like, make myself throw up after eating. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics to try to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Um… no, not really. I don’t use those to try to lose weight or anything.\n\nI mean, I know what they are, but that’s not something I do. Is that… is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is low even when others tell you it is not?\n\nPatient: I mean… people do say that, yeah. They say it’s too low or that it’s dangerous, but I guess it feels kind of exaggerated to me.\n\nI don’t really see it that way. It feels more like everyone’s focusing on my body like it’s the problem, when I’m just trying to stay disciplined and sharp for school and my work. Is that… does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Did you intentionally try to lose weight?\n\nPatient: I… I guess I did, at least at first. I don’t know if I thought of it as “trying to lose weight” exactly. It felt more like trying to be in control.\n\nLike, I plan my day around not really eating much—coffee before studio, maybe a few bites of something from the café if other people are around. And I avoid bread or anything oily because it feels like it would kind of undo the control I worked for.\n\nThat sounds bad when I say it out loud, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you mean, I guess.\n\nLike, I’m usually pretty aware of what I’m eating. I might feel upset afterward if I had more than I planned, but it doesn’t feel like I’m out of control exactly. More like I didn’t do it “right,” if that makes sense.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that's definitely larger than what most people would eat under similar circumstances?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not definitely larger than what other people would eat.\n\nSometimes I worry something was “too much” for me, but if I try to compare it to a normal person, it probably wouldn’t look like some huge amount. I’m not sure if I’m answering that right.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that you couldn't stop or control?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I’ve felt guilty or anxious after eating, but not like I couldn’t stop myself.\n\nIf anything, I’m usually kind of watching it the whole time, maybe too much. So… no, I don’t think I’ve had that feeling of being unable to control it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your anxiety about gaining weight is one of the main things that drives you to restrict your food intake?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it probably is.\n\nI don’t want to sound dramatic, but even the idea of gaining, like, a pound can make me really anxious. If I eat a normal dinner or something with butter in it, I start thinking people will somehow be able to tell… like they’ll see I’ve lost discipline or something.\n\nI know that might not make sense, but that fear is a big part of why I hold back with food.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time, and then felt really upset or guilty about it?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I definitely feel upset or guilty after eating sometimes, but I wouldn’t say it’s after a large amount in a short period of time.\n\nIt’s more like even a normal amount can feel wrong to me, if that makes sense. But I don’t think I’ve had what you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten a large amount of food in a very short time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not a large amount, no.\n\nSometimes it feels like a lot to me, but I think if someone else saw it, they probably wouldn’t call it that. I kind of immediately zoom in on my arms or my waist, and if I look small enough, I feel… relieved, I guess. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics to try to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Um… no, not really. I don’t use those to try to lose weight or anything.\n\nI mean, I know what they are, but that’s not something I do. Is that… is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is low even when others tell you it is not?\n\nPatient: I mean… people do say that, yeah. They say it’s too low or that it’s dangerous, but I guess it feels kind of exaggerated to me.\n\nI don’t really see it that way. It feels more like everyone’s focusing on my body like it’s the problem, when I’m just trying to stay disciplined and sharp for school and my work. Is that… does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Did you intentionally try to lose weight?\n\nPatient: I… I guess I did, at least at first. I don’t know if I thought of it as “trying to lose weight” exactly. It felt more like trying to be in control.\n\nLike, I plan my day around not really eating much—coffee before studio, maybe a few bites of something from the café if other people are around. And I avoid bread or anything oily because it feels like it would kind of undo the control I worked for.\n\nThat sounds bad when I say it out loud, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you mean, I guess.\n\nLike, I’m usually pretty aware of what I’m eating. I’m not sure if I’m answering that right.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that you couldn't stop or control?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I’ve felt guilty or anxious after eating, but not like I couldn’t stop myself.\n\nIf anything, I’m usually kind of watching it the whole time, maybe too much. So… no, I don’t think I’ve had that feeling of being unable to control it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your anxiety about gaining weight is one of the main things that drives you to restrict your food intake?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it probably is.\n\nI don’t want to sound dramatic, but even the idea of gaining, like, a pound can make me really anxious. But I don’t think I’ve had what you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten a large amount of food in a very short time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not a large amount, no.\n\nSometimes it feels like a lot to me, but I think if someone else saw it, they probably wouldn’t call it that. So… I don’t think I’ve eaten a large amount in a really short time. But I don’t think I’ve had what you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten a large amount of food in a very short time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not a large amount, no.\n\nSometimes it feels like a lot to me, but I think if someone else saw it, they probably wouldn’t call it that. So… I don’t think I’ve eaten a large amount in a really short time. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is your goal in restricting your food intake to be thin, rather than just to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, but I don’t like saying it that directly.\n\nIt’s not only “don’t gain weight.” I think there is this part of me that wants to look… small. Like, when I look at photos from a showcase or something, I don’t really look at the artwork first, which sounds awful. I zoom in on my arms and my waist, and if I look small enough, I feel like I seem more serious or put-together.\n\nI know that probably sounds shallow. I’m not trying to be vain, but… yeah, I think being thin feels like proof I’m doing something right. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I know I might use the word “a lot” sometimes, but it’s more like it feels like a lot to me, not that it actually is a large amount in a short time.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve had that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating and consumed a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that way.\n\nI mean, I can feel really uncomfortable or guilty after eating something, and in my head it can feel like I “lost control,” but if you mean actually eating a large amount and not being able to stop, I don’t think that’s happened. At least not that I can clearly point to.\n\nI’m sorry, I’m trying to answer it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you currently think that you are underweight?\n\nPatient: I… not really, no.\n\nI know other people keep saying it’s dangerous, and I’m not trying to dismiss them, but it feels a little exaggerated to me. Like they’re making my body the main problem, when I’m just trying to stay sharp for school and my work.\n\nMaybe that sounds defensive. I just don’t really see myself that way."
